Across the room, Selene had begun a speech about the future. Below them, someone had hidden a woman behind a number.","Cassian came to her side carrying two glasses of soda. He saw the photograph, set both down, and turned his body to screen the repair case from the room.","\"Ward C,\" Mara said.","\"The old route?\"","She nodded. The unlabelled circle on Elena's plan had to lie below the gallery's lower chamber. Her mother's instructions had led them here before Mara knew what she was looking for.","She could feel herself wanting the woman to be Elena. Wanting was fierce, hungry, and potentially stupid. The hammer mark might have travelled through another restorer's hands. A silver petal was not a parent.","Cassian looked at her face. \"We go and see.\"","Three ordinary words. She could have kissed him for them, so she did.","\"You are becoming a public nuisance,\" he murmured.","\"A celebrated one.\"","June arrived with an empty tray. \"If your career in romance can tolerate an interruption, Harrow has asked the guard to watch the service stairs.\"","Mara glanced towards the gallery. A woman in an oversize hat was telling a donor that the conservatory created a level playing field for young scientists. Behind her, a waiter was being scolded for bringing in the wrong mineral water.","\"I have a plan,\" Mara said.","\"Naturally,\" Cassian replied. \"Should I be afraid of the hat?\"","\"Only if it becomes airborne.\"","She approached the donor with her most admiring expression. \"Your hat is marvelous. Did they make it specifically for the installation?\"","The woman looked offended. \"It's a crown.\"","\"Of course. The glass butterfly is impressive.\"","The donor touched the ornament. \"It's Dutch.\"","\"Then you must see the specimen case. They have a butterfly from the same region. It would make a splendid photograph.\"","A minute later, the hat had become stuck in a hanging vine. Mara's attempt to free it brought down a decorative cage, which hit the edge of a copper basin and sent a cascade of daffodils onto Harrow's shoes.","She was sorry about the flowers. She was not sorry about the colonel.","\"Negligence!\" the donor cried.","\"Please don't move,\" Mara said. \"The crown's inner wire is caught. We don't want to inflict an injury.\"","June managed the crowd with the confidence of a priest confronting a minor eclipse. Cassian held the basin away from the donor's face. Harrow called for a ladder.","Mara slipped through the gallery door with the donor's spare cloak over her arm and Peta's key card against her palm. No magical disguise was necessary. A woman carrying someone's expensive clothes belonged, in many cases, to the landscape nobody looked at.","The service stairs were empty. She descended to the place where the air smelled of wet clay. Her shoes made a soft snap against the stone. At the first landing she found an oak door instead of the metal gate shown on the public plan.","The card worked.","Cassian joined her before the door closed. His tie was crooked and a daffodil clung to his sleeve.","\"Your admirer sends her thanks,\" he said.","\"Did she keep her crown?\"","\"With substantial spiritual damage.\"","He plucked the flower from his coat and put it behind Mara's ear. The ridiculous tenderness of the gesture nearly undid her. She took his hand and led him into the passage.","The walls curved around a circular pit covered by a brass grille. Pipes crossed beneath it. At their centre stood an old valve wheel, like the throne of a small, unpleasant emperor. Mara recognised the shape from Elena's pencil plan.","KEEP THE OLD ROUTE.","The route was a narrow maintenance ledge beneath the newer flooring. Someone had tried to lock it away with a plywood panel, but the wood had warped in the humidity. Mara loosened a nail. Cassian caught the panel as it came free.","\"Your mother had a determined approach to access,\" he whispered.","\"She disliked being confined to other people's doors.\"","Beyond the panel, a ladder led to Ward C. A bird had made its nest in the outer air vent. It fluttered above them, then vanished into darkness. Mara could hear water moving somewhere beyond the wall.","Halfway up, the ladder ended beside a second grille. A security camera hovered above it, its little red light moving through a narrow radius. Mara studied the back-and-forth sweep.","\"An optional complication,\" she whispered.","\"We could defer it.\"","\"And spend the night with the bird?\"","\"Its hospitality is unchallenged.\"","She took the donor's glass butterfly from the cloak pocket. One wing had broken during the crown's rescue. Held at the right angle, the remaining glass reflected the camera's own light back into its eye.","Cassian looked impressed. \"That will confuse it?\"","\"For a few seconds. Don't overrate contemporary engineering. In many cases it's as vain as its owners.\"","She waited for the lens to turn, raised the glass, and moved. Cassian climbed after her. The camera paused on a blurred flare of red before resuming its patrol.","At the next landing he put his hands on either side of her waist and lifted her from the ladder onto the ledge. She caught his shoulders. For a moment they stood pressed together in the warm air, with no space for the careful dignity he wore upstairs.","\"Are you admiring my work?\" she asked.","\"Partly.\"","\"Concentrate.\"","\"An unreasonable restriction.\"","His mouth brushed the corner of hers. She almost forgot what the hammer mark had made her hope. Then a distant door closed, and the moment snapped back into the serious shape of the night.","Mara touched his cheek once. \"In the near future.\"","\"I'll put it in the timetable.\"","They crossed the ledge. Below, a maintenance screen showed the old research inlet running into the public branch. Someone had overwritten a warning with the keyword NORMAL. Mara photographed it before the display could freeze again.","At the top, a small screen displayed a timetable of patient visits. There were no names. Her eye found 43 beside a blue tag and the word AVAILABLE.","The commonness of the word made her angry. A person could be sleeping, frightened, recovering, bored. Available belonged to a spare chair.","A nurse sat behind a desk with a kettle and a half-eaten biscuit. She looked up as they entered.","Cassian held out the cloak. \"Dr. Ward asked us to bring this to the lady in forty-three. Her photograph was sent upstairs.\"","He did not say they were staff. He let the woman's own hurried assumption build the rest.","\"She's in the inner room. Five minutes. No equipment.\"","Mara walked down the corridor. She counted doors because concentration was the only thing keeping her feet steady. One. Two. A storage chamber. Another door with a screen that had frozen on a blue error message.","Forty-three stood at the end.","Inside, the woman from the laboratory sat beside a narrow window. Her mask had been removed. She held a broken silver petal in a napkin, moving her thumb over its rounded edge.","Mara stopped in the doorway.","Five years changed a face. Illness altered its geography. Her mother's cheeks had grown thin, and one eyelid drooped slightly. The hair Mara remembered as black now held broad streaks of silver.","But the woman turned the petal between finger and thumb, tested its balance, and frowned at a flawed joint.","Elena had made that expression at kettles, doors, damaged toys, and sometimes her daughter's most elaborate lies.","\"You shouldn't stand in the draught,\" she said.","Mara could not make a sound.","The woman looked up. Her gaze moved over the gown, the dirt on its hem, the daffodil behind Mara's ear. A confused smile appeared and faded.","\"I asked for the restorer,\" she said.","Mara took out the flower. She opened it in her palm.","The woman stared.","A long, terrible moment passed. Mara could hear the air vent, Cassian's breathing, the faint back-and-forth of a cleaner's brush outside.","Then Elena said her name.","Not a question. Not a guess from a picture.","\"Mara.\"","Mara crossed the room and knelt before the chair. The woman touched her face with trembling fingers. Her hand was warm. Warmth seemed an impossible, mighty thing.","\"I thought—\" Mara began.","The rest would not come. For years she had told people her mother was dead with the easy, practised voice required to keep them from being uncomfortable. Now the sentence had nowhere to go.","Elena's face crumpled. \"I tried to tell them.\"","Mara pressed her cheek to that familiar hand. \"You're here.\"","\"I don't know how long.\"","\"It doesn't matter right now.\"","It mattered desperately. It would matter tomorrow, and the next month, and every time Mara tried to imagine what the years had been like. Mara had imagined, when grief was new and cruel, what she would say if she could have one more hour. She had prepared apologies, accusations, and sentences too large for any living person. His embarrassment was unfairly effective.","Elena asked about the shop. Mara told her about the leaking ceiling, the new workbench, and Admiral's determination to steal every shoe in Cassian's house. The past had not become harmless. It had, for a little while, stopped being a graveyard.","The pleasure faded as Elena grew tired. Her fingers loosened around the flower. She had entered on behalf of an independent patient-support service. Peta had given her the public ward contact; Holt was following with the necessary authority to request an assessment. Neither had been told they were attending a confirmed reunion.","Noor's eyes widened when she saw the two women. Then her professional steadiness returned like a light being switched on.","\"Hello, Elena. I'm Noor. Mara asked me to help.\"","She sat where Elena could see her without turning. She asked about pain, breathing, and whether she knew where she was. Elena answered some questions readily. Others took time. Once, she called Noor by a name nobody recognised, then corrected herself with visible distress.","\"We don't need to hurry,\" Noor said.","Mara watched every small movement. She had wanted her mother back as a single impossible gift. Now the gift contained exhaustion, confusion, and years she could neither return nor repair.","Cassian stood behind her chair. It was convincing enough to make two nurses hurry into the corridor.","Mara wheeled Elena out behind them beneath a pale hospital blanket. Peta walked at her shoulder with a folder. Noor followed with a small bag, and Cassian took the rear. For the first thirty metres they looked like an ordinary movement between wards.","Then the alarm changed.","A red light began to pulse above the doors. The nurse at the far desk looked up from her screen.","\"All transfers are on hold.\"","\"Ventilation failure,\" Peta said. \"Ward C has to clear.\"","\"I haven't had that instruction.\"","\"Then stay here and breathe the vapour.\"","It was unkind, efficient, and unlike Peta. The nurse moved to the phone. Mara pushed the wheelchair through the closing door before the explanation could catch up.","Elena gripped the blanket. \"I used to work here,\" she murmured.","\"You can show me later,\" Mara said. \"Now I need you to stay warm.\"","They entered a tunnel sloping towards the old reservoir. Cold water ran along a narrow channel in the floor. Above it, a continuous pipe carried the greenhouse return. The hum of machinery rose through the concrete like the breathing of an enormous animal.","Elena looked towards the sound. Her body stiffened.","\"Not that way.\"","Mara stopped the chair.","Peta checked the map. \"The external exit is ahead.\"","\"The bench,\" Elena said. \"They take you that way.\"","She could not explain more. Her breath had become quick and shallow. Noor crouched in front of her, blocking the view of the pipework, while Mara looked at the doors.","One led to an agricultural storage chamber. Another was marked RESEARCH RETURN. The third carried no sign, only an old-fashioned keyhole.","Mara knelt. The lock was primitive beside the electronic controls elsewhere. Its mechanism had been repaired more than once, and the lower hinge bore a small scratch in the shape of a leaf.","She touched it. Elena had used that mark on the tools she kept for herself.","\"The old route,\" Mara whispered.","The key in Peta's folder did not fit. Mara took out her tools.","\"We are in a hurry,\" Cassian said.","\"Then stop looking so encouraging. It interferes with my discipline.\"","The lock opened on the fourth movement. Beyond it stood a narrow service lift, its metal cage large enough for a cart and one worker. Mara measured it with her eyes.","The wheelchair would fit. All of them would not.","Cassian looked up the shaft. \"I'll take the stairs.\"","\"Where do they come out?\"","\"I don't know yet.\"","\"Then we keep the doors visible. No heroic disappearance.\"","He met her eyes. \"Agreed.\"","Peta went with him to show the junction. Noor entered the lift beside Elena while Mara held the gate. At the last moment her mother reached for her hand.","\"Not alone,\" Elena said.","\"You're not. Noor is going with you. I'll be at the top.\"","The promise frightened Mara because it was specific enough to fail. She watched the lift rise, then ran up the stairs after Cassian.","The staircase had been cut into the quarry's bedrock. A fossil shell showed through one rough patch, beside a modern cable fixed with plastic clips. The old geology and the new machine occupied the same wall without either explaining the other.","Mara heard footsteps below. She stopped, lifted a hand, and drew Cassian into a shallow recess behind a stack of waterproof covers. Peta followed. A security worker came through the lower door carrying a torch.","\"Can't see a thing in this mist,\" he called into his radio. \"The return line's venting again.\"","The torch beam passed across the covers. Cassian's arm tightened around Mara, holding her still against him. She could feel the fast beat of his pulse at her back. He was afraid too; the knowledge made his steadiness less distant and much more precious.","The worker climbed two steps, then stopped. Water dripped onto his head. He swore and retreated, announcing that he would take the outdoor route.","Mara waited until the door shut before breathing properly.","\"I could have distracted him,\" Peta whispered.","\"You could also have been recognised,\" Cassian said. \"We prefer you unarrested.\"","\"A positive performance review. My first this year.\"","Mara smiled despite herself. Peta's courage had the practical shape of someone going to work without expecting to become heroic. Her salary did not cover this staircase. No award would make up for the consequences if they failed.","At the first landing, they found a locked glass door. Beyond it lay a room full of tall equipment racks. Small lights moved across the panels in a pattern too orderly to be an alarm.","Peta gave a sharp breath. \"That shouldn't be connected.\"","A cable crossed the ceiling from a water-monitoring box to the communications rack. Its spiral emblem matched the ship's components. Below it, a screen displayed numbered contact stations.","Forty-three was green.","Mara turned towards Peta. \"Her cuff is off.\"","\"The chair contact,\" Peta said. \"They register the station, not the person.\"","She pointed at a short delay field. The display had not yet updated. A moment later forty-three turned grey.","Cassian filmed the transition. \"The network knows whether the contact station is occupied.\"","\"It knows whether the circuit reports contact,\" Peta replied. \"That is not always the same thing.\"","Footsteps sounded below. Ivo's diversion had reached its climax.","Mara opened the glass door with the card. Peta hurried to a terminal and copied the visible station list to a removable drive she carried for plant data. Cassian watched the stairs. Mara photographed the physical cable routes and the labels at both ends.","She had no desire to become a scholar of the machine. She wanted enough of its working parts preserved that somebody independent could test what she had seen.","An instruction flashed on the screen: REMOTE SESSION PENDING. LOCAL CONTACT REQUIRED.","Mara felt a cold understanding settle under her skin. The wall was not a magic mirror. Someone had built a way to reach the people already placed against the machinery.","A second field opened beneath the warning. It showed a paired-node number, a contact test, and the words RECENT INTERVAL ONLY. Mara photographed it, then moved close enough to catch the small print beside the cable socket.","\"Don't touch it,\" Peta whispered.","\"I'm not. The fitting has a supplier's mark.\"","She angled the phone until the mark became legible. The camera caught her own reflection in the glass: damp hair, a torn sleeve, eyes too wide. For once she did not hate the frightened woman in the picture. The woman had come this far and was still doing the next useful thing.","Peta pulled out the drive. \"That's all.\"","A man appeared at the lower landing. Cassian stepped into view before he could see the terminal.","\"Ventilation inspection,\" he said.","\"You aren't—\"","Cassian slammed the stair door between them. The man struck it with his shoulder. Mara and Peta ran towards the upper exit while Cassian braced the door long enough to give them a few seconds.","\"Go!\"","Mara stopped at the top step. \"Cassian.\"","He looked up. She pointed to a heavy trolley beside him.","He grinned, shoved it beneath the door handle, and followed her.","They emerged into a botanical store full of damp seed trays. Noor stood beside the lift, one hand on Elena's shoulder. The woman was shivering. A portable heater glowed near her feet.","Mara dropped to her knees beside the chair. \"I told you.\"","Elena touched her hair. \"Always making trouble.\"","\"Biologically inherited.\"","Her mother laughed weakly. Noor checked her pulse and looked towards the exit.","\"We need the car. Now.\"","Peta led them through a greenhouse used to irrigate juvenile plants. A shallow pool reflected the moon. Rain tapped the glass roof. In the next chamber, bright solar lamps simulated an everlasting summer over rows of organic greens.","A guard stood at the far door.","Mara pulled a large canvas tent sheet from a rack and threw it over a spare cart. Cassian helped her enclose the wheelchair beneath it, leaving the sides open for air. Peta arranged sample crates along the outer edge.","\"A delivery?\" Noor asked.","\"Agricultural specimens,\" Mara said. \"Very precious ones.\"","She pushed. Cassian took the other handle.","The guard looked at the covered load. \"What's the transaction code?\"","Peta gave him a number with magnificent confidence. It corresponded to a genuine order; he found it on his screen and waved them towards the loading lane.","Mara could see the outdoor light. June's van stood beyond the gate. For one wonderful second, escape looked almost ordinary.","Then the guard frowned.","\"That order was completed an hour ago.\"","He reached for the radio at his
